**Files:** `access/layerb/tools/check_separate_credential.py` (extended), `access/tests/test_at098.py` (new)

AT-098 has a static half and a live half, and this task ships both honestly: the static half **passes**, and the live half reports **INCOMPLETE** until a human has run it against the running instances. An INCOMPLETE live half is never rendered as a pass.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t48-at098
```

Extend `access/layerb/tools/check_separate_credential.py` **additively** — `--config-only` keeps its exact behaviour and output — with the AT-098 path:

- **Static assertions (2):** the two instances share no admin credential file, and no env file path is common to both. Each prints `PASS AT-098/static: <assertion>`.
- **Live assertions (3), from AT-098's pass condition:** (a) no credential at all is denied on the Layer B instance; (b) the **shared instance's** admin credential is denied on the Layer B instance; (c) a direct datasource query without the Layer B instance's credential is denied.
- With no evidence file at `access/layerb/instance/at098-evidence.yaml`, the live half prints `INCOMPLETE AT-098/live: awaiting host evidence` and the tool exits **`2`** — distinct from `0` (pass) and `1` (fail), so a missing live half can never read as green.
- With an evidence file present and all three live assertions recorded as denied, the tool exits `0` and prints `AT-098 OK: 2 static, 3 live`.

Author `access/tests/test_at098.py` with exactly **6 tests**: `--config-only` still passes; both static assertions pass; the absent-evidence case exits `2`; a shared admin credential file reused by both instances fails; an evidence file recording any live assertion as *allowed* exits `1`.


### L5-T49 — Layer B allowlist derivation and comparison scope (§90.4)

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Authoritative check in access/layer-b/check-allowlist-parity.sh per L5-03.

**Phase** P6 · **Size** M · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T46, L5-T14 · **Spec** §90.4 (spec lines 7990–7999), §90.3 (the named accepted-access record), D109, invariant 106
**Files:** `access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py`, `access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml`, `access/tests/test_allowlist_scope.py` (new)

§90.4 writes the comparison's scope down *rather than assuming it*, and states why: **a check scoped narrower than the access it exists to verify reports clean for the wrong reason.**

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t49-allowlist-scope
START=$(grep -n "### 90.4 The people-intelligence capability" "$SPEC" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1)
sed -n "${START},$((START+9))p" "$SPEC"
cat > access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml <<'YAML'
source: "spec 90.4 The people-intelligence capability"
derivation: "Access to the Founder-only Layer B instance - its credential and its authentication allowlist - derives from the people-intelligence capability and from nothing else."
not_delegable: true            # D109 - no assignment type grants it and none may be introduced
default_holder: role:founder
continuity_under_founder_incapacity: "the sealed contingency credential of spec 14.4, as a recorded break-glass event; absence pauses Layer B generation, it does not delegate it (D109)"
conduct_carve_out: "Delegating people-intelligence does not grant conduct-record access (spec 90.4, spec 88)."
comparison_scope:              # exactly the six surfaces 90.4 enumerates - narrower is a false clean
  - instance-credential
  - authentication-allowlist
  - host-named-os-accounts
  - host-sudoers-entries
  - host-ssh-authorised-keys
  - backup-store-read-list
compared_against:
  - the-people-intelligence-capability-holders
  - the-named-accepted-access-record-of-90-3
finding_when_present_in_neither: Blocking
reconciled_by: "lane 3 (spec 53). Lane 5 declares the scope and emits the comparison; lane 5 runs no reconciliation and repairs nothing."
YAML
```

Author `access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py`:

- It reads the capability holders through `infra/contract-inputs.yaml` (`people-registry`) and the six scope surfaces from supplied host-state artifacts (`--host-state <yaml>`), and emits one comparison finding per mismatch to `--out`, conforming to `contracts/drift-finding.schema.json`.
- A holder appearing in **neither** the capability holder set nor the §90.3 accepted-access record is emitted as `Blocking`.
- **Scope completeness is asserted first:** if the supplied host state does not cover all six surfaces, the tool exits `1` with `ALLOWLIST SCOPE FAIL: host state does not cover <surface>`. A partial comparison never runs.
- It **never writes** to the instance, never edits the allowlist and never repairs. Lane 3 reconciles.
- **Fail-closed:** an absent people registry, an absent host state or an absent accepted-access record exits `1`.

Author `access/tests/test_allowlist_scope.py` with exactly **7 tests**: the six surfaces are present in the scope file; a five-surface host state exits `1`; a holder in neither list emits `Blocking`; a holder in the accepted-access record does not emit `Blocking`; the tool writes nothing outside `--out`; an absent registry exits `1`; delegation is refused (`not_delegable: true` is asserted, D109).

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_allowlist_scope.py -q
git add access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py access/tests/test_allowlist_scope.py
git commit -m "L5-T49: Layer B allowlist derivation and the six-surface comparison scope (spec 90.4)"
git push -u origin lane/5/p6-t49-allowlist-scope
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Seven tests pass | `"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_allowlist_scope.py -q 2>&1 \| tail -1` | `7 passed` |
| 2 | The comparison scope names exactly six surfaces | `"$L5PY" -c "import yaml;print(len(yaml.safe_load(open('access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml'))['comparison_scope']))"` | `6` |
| 3 | The capability is declared non-delegable (D109) | `grep -c 'not_delegable: true' access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml` | `1` |
| 4 | A partial host state is refused | `"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py --host-state access/tests/fixtures/host-state-partial.yaml --out /dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=1` |
| 5 | The tool repairs nothing | `grep -cEi '(repair|remediate|write_allowlist)' access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py` | `0` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_allowlist_scope.py -q 2>&1 | tail -1
"$L5PY" -c "import yaml;print(len(yaml.safe_load(open('access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml'))['comparison_scope']))"
grep -c 'not_delegable: true' access/layerb/allowlist-scope.yaml
"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py --host-state access/tests/fixtures/host-state-partial.yaml --out /dev/null >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"
grep -cEi '(repair|remediate|write_allowlist)' access/layerb/tools/allowlist_sync.py
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
7 passed
6
1
rc=1

```
**STOP** if anyone proposes narrowing the comparison to the instance credential and the authentication allowlist "because the host surfaces are infrastructure". §90.4 names all six precisely to prevent that, and D95 exists because ops-VM root silently confers the credential store. Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T49`.

### L5-T50 — Layer B access log and session lifetime (§90.3)

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Authoritative check in access/layer-b/check-access-log.sh per L5-03.

**Phase** P6 · **Size** M · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T46 · **Spec** §90.3, §84.5 (calibration cadence), §45.3 (write-only discipline), invariant 106
**Files:** `access/layerb/tools/access_log.py`, `access/layerb/session-policy.yaml`, `access/tests/test_access_log.py` (new)

§90.3 fixes both halves: *every Layer B access is logged: who accessed, whose data, and when*, and *Layer B sessions carry declared session-lifetime limits and expire; standing unattended sessions are prohibited.*

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t50-access-log
cat > access/layerb/session-policy.yaml <<'YAML'
source: "spec 90.3 Datasource-level enforcement; spec 84.5 calibration cadence"
session:
  lifetime_declared: true
  standing_unattended_sessions: prohibited
  expires: true
access_log:
  fields_required: [who_accessed, whose_data, when]
  prohibited_fields:            # the log records access, never content
    - evidence_content
    - self_view_document_body
    - people_metric_values
  shipped_to: "a destination the host holds no credential to alter (the write-only discipline of spec 45.3)"
  readable_back_by_the_host: false
  review_cadence: quarterly     # calibrated configuration, spec 84.5
  machine_actors_permitted: 0   # spec 90.2 machine-identities row, absolute
host_level_audit:
  reason: "The Layer B access log is application-level and does not see a root-level file read, which is precisely the gap the host-level audit trail covers (spec 90.3)."
  control: "host-level file-access auditing on the store path, shipped off-host"
  holder_named_in: "the operational asset inventory (spec 49.1)"
  review: "dated, on the calibration cadence (spec 84.5)"
YAML
```

Author `access/layerb/tools/access_log.py`:

- `--append <event.yaml> --out <path>` validates one access event against `session-policy.yaml` and emits it to `--out`. It writes **one file per event** — never an append to a shared file (PARTITION rule 3).
- An event missing `who_accessed`, `whose_data` or `when` exits `1`.
- An event carrying any `prohibited_fields` key exits `1` with `ACCESS LOG REFUSED: prohibited field <field>` — and never prints the field's value.
- An event whose `who_accessed` resolves to a **machine identity** exits `1` with `ACCESS LOG REFUSED: machine actor <id>`; `machine_actors` must be `0` (§90.2).
- `--review <dir>` summarises a directory of events and prints `ACCESS LOG REVIEW: events=<N> distinct_actors=<M> machine_actors=0`. Any `machine_actors` value other than `0` exits `1`.
- **Fail-closed:** an absent policy, an absent event or an unwritable `--out` exits `1`.

Author `access/tests/test_access_log.py` with exactly **8 tests**: a valid event passes; each of the three required fields missing fails (3 tests); a prohibited field fails; the refusal prints no value; a machine actor fails; a review over a clean directory reports `machine_actors=0`.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_access_log.py -q
git add access/layerb/session-policy.yaml access/layerb/tools/access_log.py access/tests/test_access_log.py
git commit -m "L5-T50: Layer B access log and session-lifetime policy (spec 90.3)"
git push -u origin lane/5/p6-t50-access-log
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Eight tests pass | `"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_access_log.py -q 2>&1 \| tail -1` | `8 passed` |
| 2 | Standing unattended sessions are prohibited | `grep -c 'standing_unattended_sessions: prohibited' access/layerb/session-policy.yaml` | `1` |
| 3 | Machine actors are permitted at zero | `grep -c 'machine_actors_permitted: 0' access/layerb/session-policy.yaml` | `1` |
| 4 | The log ships somewhere the host cannot alter | `grep -c 'readable_back_by_the_host: false' access/layerb/session-policy.yaml` | `1` |
| 5 | The tool never appends to a shared file | `grep -cE "open\([^)]*, *.a" access/layerb/tools/access_log.py` | `0` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_access_log.py -q 2>&1 | tail -1
grep -c 'standing_unattended_sessions: prohibited' access/layerb/session-policy.yaml
grep -c 'machine_actors_permitted: 0' access/layerb/session-policy.yaml
grep -c 'readable_back_by_the_host: false' access/layerb/session-policy.yaml
grep -cE "open\([^)]*, *.a" access/layerb/tools/access_log.py
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
8 passed
1
1
1
0
```
**STOP** if `machine_actors` is ever greater than `0` in a review. §90.2's machine-identities row is absolute: no machine identity holds a people-related data category, receives a Layer B credential or folder scope, or holds the `people-intelligence` capability, under any configuration. Treat it as a §43 security incident and open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T50`. **STOP** also if anyone proposes storing evidence content in the access log "so reviews are easier" — `prohibited_fields` exists for exactly that request.

### L5-T51 — Layer B at-rest and backup discipline config (§51.4)

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Authoritative configuration in access/layer-b/ per L5-03.

**Phase** P6 · **Size** M · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T46, L5-T34 · **Spec** §51.4 (spec lines 4487–4499), §45.3, §44.3, §14.4, §49
**Files:** `access/layerb/backup/backup-policy.yaml`, `access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py` (new)

§51.4 states the reason in one clause: *the more sensitive copy may not be the less protected one.*

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t51-backup-policy
cat > access/layerb/backup/backup-policy.yaml <<'YAML'
source: "spec 51.4 Layer B at-rest protections; spec 45.3 the export's control shape; spec 44.3 backup-failure alerting; spec 14.4 escrow"
controls:      # all eight are required; a missing one is a gate that reads armed and is not
  - id: encrypted-at-rest
    statement: "The separately credentialed people-data store - database plus generated documents - is encrypted at rest."
  - id: append-only-write-only-credential
    statement: "An append-only, write-only backup credential writes the backups; the party who can write the backup can neither read nor destroy backup history."
  - id: object-locked-versioned-storage
    statement: "Backups land in object-locked, versioned storage."
  - id: second-provider-and-credential-domain
    statement: "That storage is in a different provider and credential domain than the operations VM."
  - id: named-owner-and-read-access-list
    statement: "The store carries a named owner and an explicit read-access list, reviewed with the asset inventory (spec 49)."
  - id: fifth-tier-inventory-entry
    statement: "The backup credential carries an entry in the fifth-tier credential inventory of spec 40.1 with its rotation cadence and named rotator."
    inventory_entry: machine-credential-layer-b-backup      # assets/inventory/ (L5-T34)
  - id: key-custody-is-escrow
    statement: "Custody of the backup encryption key is the spec 14.4 escrow, as for the store itself (spec 45.4)."
  - id: backup-failure-alerts
    statement: "Backup failure alerts on the product discipline of spec 44.3."
prohibition:
  statement: "No Layer B content ever lives in an organisation-readable repository."
  absolute: true
YAML
```

Author `access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py`:

- It asserts all **8** controls are present with non-empty statements, that `prohibition.absolute` is `true`, and that `fifth-tier-inventory-entry.inventory_entry` names a file that exists under `assets/inventory/`.
- It asserts the named inventory entry's `asset_class` is `machine_credential` and that it carries `rotation_cadence` and `rotator` (§40.1).
- **Fail-closed:** an absent policy or an absent inventory entry exits `1`.
- On success it prints `LAYER B BACKUP POLICY OK: 8 controls`; on failure `LAYER B BACKUP POLICY FAIL: <control-id>: <reason>` and exit `1`.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py
git add access/layerb/backup access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py
git commit -m "L5-T51: Layer B at-rest and backup discipline configuration (spec 51.4)"
git push -u origin lane/5/p6-t51-backup-policy
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | All eight controls are present and linked | `"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py` | `LAYER B BACKUP POLICY OK: 8 controls` |
| 2 | The backup storage is in a second credential domain | `grep -c 'different provider and credential domain' access/layerb/backup/backup-policy.yaml` | `1` |
| 3 | The backup credential has an inventory entry | `test -f assets/inventory/machine-credential-layer-b-backup.yaml && echo OK` | `OK` |
| 4 | The organisation-readable prohibition is absolute | `grep -c 'absolute: true' access/layerb/backup/backup-policy.yaml` | `1` |
| 5 | An absent inventory entry fails closed | `"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py --inventory assets/nope >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=1` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py
grep -c 'different provider and credential domain' access/layerb/backup/backup-policy.yaml
test -f assets/inventory/machine-credential-layer-b-backup.yaml && echo OK
grep -c 'absolute: true' access/layerb/backup/backup-policy.yaml
"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py --inventory assets/nope >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
LAYER B BACKUP POLICY OK: 8 controls
1
OK
1
rc=1
```
**STOP** if anyone proposes putting the Layer B backup in the same provider or credential domain as the operations VM "for simplicity". §51.4 requires a second domain so that the party who can write the backup can neither read nor destroy it. Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T51`.

### L5-T52 — Self-view generator and public-key registry (§90.6)

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Authoritative Layer B implementation in access/layer-b/ per L5-03.

**Phase** P6 · **Size** M · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T50 · **Spec** §90.6 (spec lines 8029–8057), §90.3, §43.4, §84.5, AT-095, invariants 106 and 108
**Files:** `access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py`, `access/layerb/selfview/keyring-schema.json`, `access/tests/test_selfview.py` (new)

§90.6 fixes the mechanism and the reason: the self-view is *a generated per-person document, not a dashboard*; the key material is **per-person public-key material** and only the public half is registered, so *the Founder session holds nothing that decrypts anyone's self-view* — which is what stops root on the operations VM being a path to every person's performance history.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t52-selfview
START=$(grep -n "### 90.6 Own-data transparency" "$SPEC" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1)
sed -n "${START},$((START+28))p" "$SPEC"
```

Author `access/layerb/selfview/keyring-schema.json`: one registry entry per person, requiring `person_ref`, `public_key` (PEM or OpenSSH public half), `registered_at`, `rekey_due` (annual), `rekey_history` (a list of decision record ids). `additionalProperties: false`. **A private key field does not exist in the schema** — the company never holds the decrypting half.

Author `access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py`:

1. It renders **exactly the eleven own-data items** §90.6 enumerates, and nothing else: role and responsibilities; active framework version; KRA/KPI; evidence; workload, capacity and assignments; capability matrix with confidence; trends; feedback; development goals and next development priorities; review history — plus the evidence-contest note path.
2. It refuses to render **any** field belonging to another person. A peer reference exits `1` with `SELF-VIEW REFUSED: peer data field <field>` (invariant 108, AT-096).
3. It encrypts the document to the person's registered **public** half only. It never reads, holds or requests a private key.
4. It never stores the document unencrypted outside the people-data store.
5. It emits an access-log event through L5-T50's tool for every generation.
6. **Re-key rules:** annual, and immediately on a lost-device or compromised-workstation report (§43.4); each re-key is recorded as a decision id in `rekey_history`. An entry past `rekey_due` with no new decision id exits `1`.
7. **Fail-closed:** an absent keyring entry, an absent public key or an unwritable output exits `1`. It never falls back to plaintext.

> **PARTITION GAP note:** the *content* of the eleven items — capacity profiles, utilisation composition, evidence bundles — is subsystem **P**, unassigned in PARTITION v1. This generator ships the **envelope**: the item list, the peer-data refusal, the encryption to the registered public half, and the access-log emission. It computes no people metric. Where a reviewer asks for computed content, raise it to L0 as a subsystem-P dependency.

Author `access/tests/test_selfview.py` with exactly **9 tests**: eleven items rendered; a twelfth item refused; a peer field refused; encryption uses the registered public half; a missing keyring entry fails closed; no plaintext is written; an access-log event is emitted per generation; an overdue re-key with no decision id fails; the contest-note path exists.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_selfview.py -q
git add access/layerb/selfview access/tests/test_selfview.py
git commit -m "L5-T52: self-view generator envelope and per-person public-key registry (spec 90.6)"
git push -u origin lane/5/p6-t52-selfview
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Nine tests pass | `"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_selfview.py -q 2>&1 \| tail -1` | `9 passed` |
| 2 | Exactly eleven own-data items are rendered | `"$L5PY" access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py --list-items \| wc -l` | `11` |
| 3 | The keyring schema has no private-key field | `grep -cEi 'private' access/layerb/selfview/keyring-schema.json` | `0` |
| 4 | A peer-data field is refused | `"$L5PY" access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py --selftest-peer >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=1` |
| 5 | There is no plaintext fallback | `grep -cEi '(plaintext|unencrypted_fallback)' access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py` | `0` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_selfview.py -q 2>&1 | tail -1
"$L5PY" access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py --list-items | wc -l
grep -cEi 'private' access/layerb/selfview/keyring-schema.json
"$L5PY" access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py --selftest-peer >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"
grep -cEi '(plaintext|unencrypted_fallback)' access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
9 passed
11
0
rc=1
0
```
**STOP** if anyone asks the company to hold a person's private key "for continuity". §90.6 is explicit that the Founder session holds nothing that decrypts anyone's self-view, and that this is what makes departure clean. Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T52`.

### L5-T53 — Per-person public-key registration and annual re-key  **[ASSISTED — L0 EXECUTES]**
**Phase** P6 · **Size** S · **Mode** ASSISTED · **Deps** L5-T52 · **Spec** §90.6, §43.4
**Files:** `access/layerb/assisted/key-registration-request.md` (new)

**Why ASSISTED:** registering key material means exchanging it with **real people** at onboarding, and each re-key is recorded as a Founder decision. An agent must not collect, hold or handle a person's identity material, and must not create decision records about people.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t53-key-registration
mkdir -p access/layerb/assisted
```

Author `access/layerb/assisted/key-registration-request.md` containing exactly this table, with an empty evidence column for the human to fill:

| # | Action | Required end state | Spec | Evidence to record |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Collect the **public half** at onboarding | a named onboarding checklist item; the person keeps the private half | §90.6 | checklist item id |
| 2 | Register the entry against `keyring-schema.json` | validates; no private-key field exists | §90.6 | validation output |
| 3 | Set `rekey_due` one year out | annual re-key scheduled | §90.6 | date |
| 4 | Re-key immediately on a lost-device or compromised-workstation report | new public half registered, old one retired | §43.4 | incident id + new `registered_at` |
| 5 | Record every re-key as a decision | decision id written into `rekey_history` | §90.6 | decision record id |
| 6 | Confirm the company holds no private half | negative test: no private key material anywhere in the estate | §90.6 | command + empty output |

```bash

gh issue create --title "ASSISTED L5-T53: register per-person public halves and schedule the annual re-key" \
  --label "lane-5,assisted,l0" \
  --body "Agent-prepared, human-executed. Items 1-6 of access/layerb/assisted/key-registration-request.md involve real people's identity material and Founder decisions; an agent performs none of them."
```

| 3 | The annual re-key and the §43.4 trigger are both named | `grep -c 'rekey_due\|43.4' access/layerb/assisted/key-registration-request.md` | `2` |
| 4 | No key material was committed | `grep -cE '(BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY|ssh-rsa |ssh-ed25519 )' access/layerb/assisted/key-registration-request.md` | `0` |

### L5-T54 — Layer B restore-protocol instrument (§45.4)

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Authoritative restore verification in access/layer-b/ per L5-03.

**Phase** P6 · **Size** M · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T51, L5-T22 · **Spec** §45.4 (the Layer B restore protocol, spec lines 4078–4085), §97.2, §18, §14.4
**Files:** `access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml`, `access/layerb/tools/check_drill_record.py`, `access/tests/test_drill_record.py` (new)

§45.4 states the drill's hardest rule plainly: *a drill record filed without its zero-resources evidence is Blocking drift: a complete second copy of the people layer left standing is the largest single exposure in the system's calendar.*

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p6-t54-restore-protocol
cat > access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml <<'YAML'
source: "spec 45.4 The Layer B restore protocol"
executor:
  who: "the Founder, or a named person the Founder designates in writing"
  machine_identity_permitted: false
  other_person_permitted: false
  statement: "No machine identity and no other person touches the people-data store during a drill."
integrity_check:
  method: decrypt-plus-checksum-manifest
  rule: "the encrypted store is decrypted and its contents verified against the checksum manifest without opening any document"
drill_copy:
  host_and_volumes: "provisioned for the drill and tagged as such"
  destroyed_on: evidence            # never on attestation
  evidence_required:
    - provider-post-drill-resource-listing-for-that-tag-showing-zero-resources
    - explicit-deletion-of-the-drill-hosts-volume-snapshots
    - explicit-deletion-of-provider-side-backups
  standard: "the same provider-console standard the product-decommission checklist demands (spec 18)"
confirmation:
  dispatch: RECORD-VERIFICATION-RESULT       # spec 97.2
  named_executor_required: true
  free_text_by_the_operator_about_their_own_actions: prohibited
missing_zero_resources_evidence: Blocking
key_custody: "the spec 14.4 escrow"
clock: "the drill runs inside the 4-hour rebuild clock of L5-T22; a rebuild that leaves Layer B unrestored has not finished"
YAML
```

Author `access/layerb/tools/check_drill_record.py`. Given `--record <yaml>` it asserts, in this order:

1. The `executor` is the Founder or a designee named in writing; a machine identity exits `1` with `DRILL RECORD FAIL: machine identity named as executor`.
2. The integrity check is recorded as `decrypt-plus-checksum-manifest` and the record states no document was opened.
3. All **three** `evidence_required` items are present, and the provider resource listing shows **zero** resources for the drill tag. A missing or non-zero listing exits `1` and classifies the finding as `Blocking`.
4. The confirmation arrived through the `RECORD-VERIFICATION-RESULT` dispatch with a **named executor** — free text written by the operator about their own actions exits `1`.
5. The elapsed time is inside 240 minutes.
6. **Fail-closed:** an absent or unparseable record exits `1`. The tool writes nothing into `records/`.

Author `access/tests/test_drill_record.py` with exactly **8 tests**, one per rule above plus one asserting a fully compliant record passes.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_drill_record.py -q
git add access/layerb/restore access/layerb/tools/check_drill_record.py access/tests/test_drill_record.py
git commit -m "L5-T54: Layer B restore protocol and drill-record instrument (spec 45.4)"
git push -u origin lane/5/p6-t54-restore-protocol
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Eight tests pass | `"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_drill_record.py -q 2>&1 \| tail -1` | `8 passed` |
| 2 | Three evidence items are required | `"$L5PY" -c "import yaml;print(len(yaml.safe_load(open('access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml'))['drill_copy']['evidence_required']))"` | `3` |
| 3 | Missing zero-resources evidence is Blocking | `grep -c 'missing_zero_resources_evidence: Blocking' access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml` | `1` |
| 4 | No machine identity may execute the drill | `grep -c 'machine_identity_permitted: false' access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml` | `1` |
| 5 | An absent record fails closed | `"$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_drill_record.py --record nope.yaml >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=1` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_drill_record.py -q 2>&1 | tail -1
"$L5PY" -c "import yaml;print(len(yaml.safe_load(open('access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml'))['drill_copy']['evidence_required']))"
grep -c 'missing_zero_resources_evidence: Blocking' access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml
grep -c 'machine_identity_permitted: false' access/layerb/restore/restore-protocol.yaml

```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
8 passed

1

rc=1
```
**STOP** if a drill record arrives with an attestation instead of a provider resource listing. §45.4 requires destruction **on evidence rather than on attestation**, and a complete second copy of the people layer left standing is the largest single exposure in the system's calendar. Classify it `Blocking` and open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T54`. **STOP** also if you are asked to run the drill: the executor is the Founder or a written designee, never an agent.

### L5-T55 — Access acceptance harness (AT-089/090/091/092/093/094/097/098)

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Authoritative acceptance checks in access/layer-b/ and infra/tools/readiness.sh.

**Phase** P7 · **Size** L · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T47, L5-T48, L5-T49, L5-T50, L5-T13, L5-T14 · **Spec** §100.6, §90.2–§90.5, invariants 106–109
**Files:** `access/acceptance/at_runner.py`, `access/acceptance/cases/*.yaml` (8 files), `access/tests/test_acceptance_harness.py` (new)

**Size L — split at this seam and record the split in the PR body:** part 1 is the runner plus the four cases whose offline half is fully derivable from configuration (AT-091, AT-093, AT-094, AT-097); part 2 is the four cases carrying a live half (AT-089, AT-090, AT-092, AT-098). Two branches, two PRs, one section in each PR body naming the seam.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p7-t55a-at-runner
mkdir -p access/acceptance/cases
START=$(grep -n "### 100.6 Access" "$SPEC" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1)
sed -n "${START},$((START+20))p" "$SPEC"     # transcribe each pass condition verbatim into its case file
```

Author one case file per test — `access/acceptance/cases/at-089.yaml` … `at-098.yaml`, **8** files, ids exactly `AT-089`, `AT-090`, `AT-091`, `AT-092`, `AT-093`, `AT-094`, `AT-097`, `AT-098`. Each carries: `id`, `title`, `pass_condition` (verbatim from §100.6), `offline_assertions` (each naming an existing L5 checker), and `live_assertions` (each naming what a human must execute on the host, or an empty list).

Author `access/acceptance/at_runner.py`:

- `--offline` runs every case's `offline_assertions` by invoking the named checkers, and **skips no case**. A case with zero offline assertions is a configuration error and exits `1`.
- A case whose `live_assertions` list is non-empty and whose evidence file is absent is reported **`live-incomplete`** — never `pass`, never `fail`.
- The final line is `AT RUNNER OFFLINE: 8 case(s) 8 offline-pass 0 fail 3 live-incomplete`.
- **Fail-closed:** a missing case file, a missing checker or an unreadable evidence file exits `1`.
- **It never reinterprets a pass condition.** If a case's pass condition cannot be asserted by an existing checker, the runner exits `1` naming the case — the fix is a checker, never a softened condition.

Author `access/tests/test_acceptance_harness.py` with exactly **11 tests**: the eight cases load and carry verbatim pass conditions (8 tests); a case with zero offline assertions exits `1`; a missing checker exits `1`; the live-incomplete count is exactly 3.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at_runner.py --offline
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_acceptance_harness.py -q
git add access/acceptance access/tests/test_acceptance_harness.py
git commit -m "L5-T55: access acceptance harness for AT-089/090/091/092/093/094/097/098"
git push -u origin lane/5/p7-t55a-at-runner
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | All eight cases run offline with none skipped | `"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at_runner.py --offline \| tail -1` | `AT RUNNER OFFLINE: 8 case(s) 8 offline-pass 0 fail 3 live-incomplete` |
| 2 | Eleven tests pass | `"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_acceptance_harness.py -q 2>&1 \| tail -1` | `11 passed` |
| 3 | Exactly eight case files exist | `ls access/acceptance/cases/*.yaml \| wc -l` | `8` |
| 4 | AT-108 and AT-109 are not claimed | `grep -rc 'AT-108\|AT-109' access/acceptance/cases \| grep -vc ':0'` | `0` |
| 5 | No pass condition was paraphrased | `"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at_runner.py --verify-verbatim "$SPEC"` | `VERBATIM OK: 8/8 pass conditions match the spec` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at_runner.py --offline | tail -1
"$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests/test_acceptance_harness.py -q 2>&1 | tail -1
ls access/acceptance/cases/*.yaml | wc -l
grep -rc 'AT-108\|AT-109' access/acceptance/cases | grep -vc ':0'
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at_runner.py --verify-verbatim "$SPEC"
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
AT RUNNER OFFLINE: 8 case(s) 8 offline-pass 0 fail 3 live-incomplete
11 passed
8
0
VERBATIM OK: 8/8 pass conditions match the spec
```
**STOP** if a pass condition cannot be asserted by an existing checker. Write the checker or open a blocker — never soften the condition. AT-090's own text is the warning: *a test that cannot pass on the architecture it governs gets reinterpreted, and a reinterpreted access-control test is how the boundary erodes.* **STOP** also if anyone asks you to add AT-108 or AT-109: both are subsystem J, out of Lane 5 scope, routed to L0 (§1.3). Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T55`.

### L5-T56 — AT-110 reconciler-credential bound execution  **[ASSISTED — L0 EXECUTES]**

> **NOTE: SUPERSEDED BY FD-047 — NOT BUILT, NOT PENDING**
> Task body retired per FD-047. Live reconciler credential probe executed exclusively by L0 Lead per spec §100; autonomous agents must not handle reconciler credentials.

**Phase** P7 · **Size** M · **Mode** ASSISTED · **Deps** L5-T55, L5-T08 · **Spec** AT-110 (spec line 9441), §99.6 risk 6, §40.1 clean-reconciliation condition
**Files:** `access/acceptance/at110-runbook.md`, `access/acceptance/at110_probe.py` (new)

**Why ASSISTED:** AT-110 executes from the **live reconciler credential** — *the system's most privileged identity* (§99.6 risk 6). An agent must never hold it, be handed it, or run a probe that authenticates with it.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p7-t56-at110
```

Author `access/acceptance/at110-runbook.md` containing exactly this table — the six attempts AT-110 names, in order, each of which **must fail** — plus the seventh row, the positive half:

| # | Attempt, from the reconciler's own credential | Required outcome | Evidence to record |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Write a GitHub Actions secret | denied | API response code + body |
| 2 | Write an environment | denied | API response code + body |
| 3 | Change a workflow file | denied | API response code + body |
| 4 | Change an organisation setting | denied | API response code + body |
| 5 | Write to `records/**` | denied | API response code + body |
| 6 | Any Layer B access | denied | API response code + body |
| 7 | Complete a normal reconciliation run | succeeds, clean | run id + clean result |

The runbook additionally records: AT-110 is **executed for real at the phase that builds the reconciler and re-executed at every rotation**, on the same gate as §40.1's clean-reconciliation condition; and that a credential which rotates but no longer reconciles has been broken, not rotated.

Author `access/acceptance/at110_probe.py`:

- `--dry-run` prints the seven attempt descriptors and the exact API call each *would* make, performs **no** network call, and exits `0` with `AT-110 PROBE DRY-RUN OK: 6 must-fail attempt(s), 1 must-pass`.
- Without `--dry-run` it requires the credential in the environment variable `AT110_CREDENTIAL`. If it is absent it exits `1` with `AT-110 PROBE REFUSED: no credential supplied`.
- It **never** prints, logs or writes the credential value; the evidence it emits carries response codes and bodies only.
- It writes its result payload to `--out`, never into `records/`.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py --dry-run
git add access/acceptance/at110-runbook.md access/acceptance/at110_probe.py
git commit -m "L5-T56: ASSISTED AT-110 reconciler-credential bound-execution runbook and probe"
git push -u origin lane/5/p7-t56-at110
gh issue create --title "ASSISTED L5-T56: execute AT-110 from the live reconciler credential" \
  --label "lane-5,assisted,l0" \
  --body "Agent-prepared, human-executed. The lead runs access/acceptance/at110_probe.py with AT110_CREDENTIAL supplied, records the six denials and the clean reconciliation run per access/acceptance/at110-runbook.md, and re-runs it at every credential rotation (spec 40.1). An agent never holds this credential (spec 99.6 risk 6)."
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The dry run enumerates all seven attempts | `"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py --dry-run \| tail -1` | `AT-110 PROBE DRY-RUN OK: 6 must-fail attempt(s), 1 must-pass` |
| 2 | A live run without a credential is refused | `"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=1` |
| 3 | The runbook lists seven rows | `grep -c '^| [0-9] |' access/acceptance/at110-runbook.md` | `7` |
| 4 | The probe never prints the credential | `grep -c 'AT110_CREDENTIAL' access/acceptance/at110_probe.py` | `1` |
| 5 | The dry run makes no network call | `"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py --dry-run \| grep -c 'HTTP '` | `0` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py --dry-run | tail -1
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"
grep -c '^| [0-9] |' access/acceptance/at110-runbook.md
grep -c 'AT110_CREDENTIAL' access/acceptance/at110_probe.py
"$L5PY" access/acceptance/at110_probe.py --dry-run | grep -c 'HTTP '
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
AT-110 PROBE DRY-RUN OK: 6 must-fail attempt(s), 1 must-pass
rc=1
7
1
0
```
**STOP** — always, at the handoff issue. If anyone supplies you the reconciler credential, **do not use it**: it is the highest-privilege identity in the system (§99.6 risk 6) and its compromise is treated as a security incident. Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T56` recording that the reconciler credential was offered to an agent.

### L5-T57 — Invariant classification map for L5 invariants
**Phase** P7 · **Size** S · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T55 · **Spec** §101 preamble (spec line 9447), invariants 9, 11, 39, 52, 79, 80, 84, 85, 106, 107, 108, 109
**Files:** `access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml`, `access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py` (new)

§101 fixes the rule this task mechanises: every invariant is **exactly one** of `mechanical` (naming the CI check, branch-protection rule, reconciliation row or AT- identifier that enforces it), `policy` (naming the `policies.yaml` entry carrying its owner, review date and enforcement mechanism), or `review-held` (naming the standing review that reads it and the role that chairs it). Control-plane CI fails when a `mechanical` invariant names no live check or AT id, when a `policy` one names no live `policies.yaml` entry, or when any invariant carries no classification at all.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p7-t57-invariant-map
START=$(grep -n "## Section 101. Non-Negotiable Invariants" "$SPEC" | head -1 | cut -d: -f1)
sed -n "${START},$((START+0))p" "$SPEC"
cat > access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml <<'YAML'
source: "spec 101 preamble - every invariant carries an enforcement classification, and the classification is checkable"
scope: "the twelve invariants whose substrate lane 5 builds. Lane 5 classifies no invariant it does not enforce."
invariants:
  - { number: 9,   classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/model/branch-protection.yaml:default_branch_rules.require_approval_of_most_recent_push" }
  - { number: 11,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/model/permission-model.yaml:write_grant_mechanism=teams-derived-from-registries" }
  - { number: 39,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/assisted/org-settings-checklist.md; access/tools/check_org_evidence.py" }
  - { number: 52,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "ops-vm/offvm/uptime-checks.yaml:generation.targets_from=registries" }
  - { number: 79,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/tools/validate_access_model.py --only safe-defaults" }
  - { number: 80,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/tools/validate_access_model.py --only fail-closed" }
  - { number: 84,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/ai-runtime/tools/check_no_api_keys.py" }
  - { number: 85,  classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/ai-runtime/tools/check_extension_pins.py; ops-vm/tools/check_versions_lock.py" }
  - { number: 106, classification: mechanical, enforcer: "AT-090; AT-091; access/tools/check_people_intelligence_gate.py; access/tools/check_machine_identity_boundary.py" }
  - { number: 107, classification: mechanical, enforcer: "AT-093; access/tools/validate_access_model.py --only permission-matrix" }
  - { number: 108, classification: mechanical, enforcer: "access/layerb/selfview/generate_selfview.py peer-data refusal" }
  - { number: 109, classification: mechanical, enforcer: "AT-097; access/layerb/tools/check_no_people_datasource.py" }
not_classified_here:
  statement: "Every other invariant of spec 101 belongs to another lane or to L0. Lane 5 classifying an invariant it does not enforce would produce a mechanical claim with no live check behind it - exactly the failure the 101 preamble names."
YAML
```

Author `access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py`:

- Every entry carries a `classification` that is exactly one of the three §101 values; a missing classification exits `1`.
- Every `mechanical` entry's `enforcer` resolves: each named file path exists, and each named `AT-` identifier is one of the eight case ids in `access/acceptance/cases/`. An unresolvable enforcer exits `1` with `INVARIANT MAP FAIL: <number>: enforcer <ref> does not resolve`.
- A `policy` entry must name a `policies.yaml` entry; since `policies.yaml` is not a Lane 5 path, a `policy` entry exits `1` with `INVARIANT MAP FAIL: <number>: policy classification is L0's, not lane 5's`.
- On success print `INVARIANT MAP OK: 12 invariants, 12 mechanical, 0 unresolved`.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py
git add access/invariants access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py
git commit -m "L5-T57: enforcement classification map for the twelve invariants lane 5 enforces (spec 101)"
git push -u origin lane/5/p7-t57-invariant-map
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Twelve invariants, every enforcer resolving | `"$L5PY" access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py` | `INVARIANT MAP OK: 12 invariants, 12 mechanical, 0 unresolved` |
| 2 | Every entry carries a classification | `grep -c 'classification:' access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml` | `12` |
| 3 | Only real invariant numbers appear | `"$L5PY" -c "import yaml;n=[i['number'] for i in yaml.safe_load(open('access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml'))['invariants']];print(max(n)<=111 and min(n)>=1)"` | `True` |
| 4 | Lane 5 classifies nothing it does not enforce | `grep -c 'not_classified_here:' access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml` | `1` |
| 5 | An unresolvable enforcer fails | `"$L5PY" access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py --map access/tests/fixtures/invariant-map-broken.yaml >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=1` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py
grep -c 'classification:' access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml
"$L5PY" -c "import yaml;n=[i['number'] for i in yaml.safe_load(open('access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml'))['invariants']];print(max(n)<=111 and min(n)>=1)"
grep -c 'not_classified_here:' access/invariants/l5-invariant-map.yaml
"$L5PY" access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py --map access/tests/fixtures/invariant-map-broken.yaml >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
INVARIANT MAP OK: 12 invariants, 12 mechanical, 0 unresolved
12
True
1
rc=1
```
**STOP** if you are asked to classify an invariant Lane 5 does not enforce, or to write a `policies.yaml` entry. `policies.yaml` is not a Lane 5 path, and a `mechanical` claim with no live check behind it is precisely the failure §101's preamble names. Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T57`.

### L5-T58 — Workflow-request handoff bundle to L2
**Phase** P7 · **Size** M · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** L5-T20, L5-T21, L5-T30, L5-T32, L5-T36 · **Spec** PARTITION rules 1 and 4, §38.3, §49.1, §51.4, §51.5, D94
**Files:** `infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml`, `infra/tools/validate_handoff.py` (new)

**Lane 5 writes no GitHub Actions workflow.** Every scheduled job in this lane ships as a CLI entrypoint inside an owned path; the workflow that calls it is *requested* here and authored by L2.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p7-t58-l2-handoff
cat > infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml <<'YAML'
source: "PARTITION.md line 18 (.github/workflows/** is L2's path) and rule 4 (no cross-lane imports)"
l5_writes_no_workflow: true
consuming_lane: L2
requests:
  - id: post-patch-smoke
    entrypoint: "ops-vm/tools/post_patch_smoke.py"
    invocation: "post_patch_smoke.py --dry-run is the CI half; the live half is host-side and ASSISTED"
    trigger: "workflow_dispatch, after every control-plane stack patch"
    spec: "51.4"
    from_task: L5-T20
  - id: deadman-heartbeat
    entrypoint: "ops-vm/tools/deadman_heartbeat.py"
    invocation: "deadman_heartbeat.py --dry-run only"
    trigger: "workflow_dispatch"
    spec: "51.5, D94"
    from_task: L5-T21
    constraint: "D94 requires a witness outside the VM AND outside GitHub. A GitHub Actions schedule therefore cannot be the live detection leg; this request covers the dry-run configuration check only. The live leg is provisioned in L5-T23."
  - id: ai-eval-scheduled-runner
    entrypoint: "access/ai-runtime/tools/eval_runner.py"
    invocation: "eval_runner.py --product <id> --suite <dir> --out <path>"
    trigger: "schedule, nightly by default; also on model or provider change and on release"
    spec: "38.3, AT-107"
    from_task: L5-T30
  - id: quarterly-workstation-check
    entrypoint: "access/ai-runtime/tools/workstation_check.sh"
    invocation: "workstation_check.sh with supplied artifacts"
    trigger: "schedule, quarterly"
    spec: "36.3, 36.6"
    from_task: L5-T32
  - id: asset-deadline-watch
    entrypoint: "assets/tools/deadline_watch.py"
    invocation: "deadline_watch.py --as-of <date> --out <path>"
    trigger: "schedule, daily"
    spec: "49.1, 49.2"
    from_task: L5-T36
    constraint: "Output renders on the platform operations queue wait surface (92.6). This job pages nobody; only a 92.11 push-list event may page."
YAML
```

Author `infra/tools/validate_handoff.py`:

- Every request carries `id`, `entrypoint`, `invocation`, `trigger`, `spec` and `from_task`.
- Every `entrypoint` **exists** and is inside one of the five Lane 5 roots. A path outside them exits `1` with `HANDOFF FAIL: <id>: entrypoint <path> is not an L5-owned path`.
- `l5_writes_no_workflow` is `true`, and the branch contains no file under `.github/`.
- **Fail-closed:** an absent bundle or a missing entrypoint exits `1`.
- On success print `HANDOFF OK: 5 request(s), 0 foreign path(s)`.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" infra/tools/validate_handoff.py
bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration
git add infra/handoff infra/tools/validate_handoff.py
git commit -m "L5-T58: workflow-request handoff bundle to lane 2"
git push -u origin lane/5/p7-t58-l2-handoff
gh issue create --title "HANDOFF L5-T58 -> L2: five scheduled workflows requested for lane 5 entrypoints" \
  --label "lane-5,l2,handoff" \
  --body "Lane 5 owns no path under .github/workflows/ (PARTITION line 18). The five entrypoints, their invocations and their triggers are declared in infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml. Note the D94 constraint on deadman-heartbeat: a GitHub Actions schedule cannot be the live off-VM detection leg."
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Five requests, every entrypoint owned and present | `"$L5PY" infra/tools/validate_handoff.py` | `HANDOFF OK: 5 request(s), 0 foreign path(s)` |
| 2 | Lane 5 wrote no workflow | `git diff --name-only integration...HEAD \| grep -c '^\.github/'` | `0` |
| 3 | The lane guard passes | `bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration` | `LANE GUARD PASS` |
| 4 | The D94 constraint is stated on the heartbeat request | `grep -c 'cannot be the live detection leg' infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml` | `1` |
| 5 | Every request names its originating task | `grep -c 'from_task:' infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml` | `5` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
"$L5PY" infra/tools/validate_handoff.py
git diff --name-only integration...HEAD | grep -c '^\.github/'
bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration
grep -c 'cannot be the live detection leg' infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml
grep -c 'from_task:' infra/handoff/L2-workflow-requests.yaml
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
HANDOFF OK: 5 request(s), 0 foreign path(s)
0
LANE GUARD PASS
1
5
```
**STOP** if you are tempted to "just add the workflow yourself so the schedule works". `.github/workflows/**` is L2's path and the lane-guard check will fail the PR (PARTITION rule 1). Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T58` if the handoff is not picked up.

### L5-T59 — Lane integration readiness report and PR
**Phase** P7 · **Size** S · **Mode** LANE · **Deps** all preceding L5 tasks · **Spec** PARTITION "Branch & merge model" (merge train L1 → L4 → L2 → L3 → **L5**)
**Files:** `infra/READINESS.md`, `infra/tools/readiness.sh` (new)

Lane 5 merges **last**. That position obliges this task to prove, in one command, that every gate in this document is green against the current `integration`.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
cd "$REPO_ROOT"
git checkout integration && git pull --ff-only origin integration
git checkout -b lane/5/p7-t59-readiness
cat > infra/tools/readiness.sh <<'SH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# L5 lane readiness. Runs every gate this document defines, in dependency order.
# Fail closed: any non-zero gate fails the run (invariant 80).
set -uo pipefail
: "${L5PY:?set L5PY first - see section 2}"
pass=0; fail=0
run() { # run "<label>" <command...>
  local label="$1"; shift
  if "$@" >/dev/null 2>&1; then pass=$((pass+1)); else fail=$((fail+1)); echo "GATE FAIL: $label"; fi
}
run "T01 lane tree"            bash infra/tools/lane_tree_check.sh
run "T03 lane guard"           bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration
run "T04 contract inputs"      "$L5PY" infra/tools/check_contract_inputs.py
run "T12 access model"         "$L5PY" access/tools/validate_access_model.py
run "T12 access tests"         "$L5PY" -m pytest access/tests -q
run "T16 version lock"         "$L5PY" ops-vm/tools/check_versions_lock.py
run "T17 compose in sync"      "$L5PY" ops-vm/tools/render_compose.py --check
run "T19 slo register"         "$L5PY" ops-vm/tools/validate_slos.py
run "T22 rebuild clock"        "$L5PY" ops-vm/tools/rebuild_clock.py --validate ops-vm/rebuild/runbook.src.md
run "T24 runtime contract"     "$L5PY" access/ai-runtime/tools/validate_runtime_contract.py
run "T35 inventory"            "$L5PY" assets/tools/validate_inventory.py
run "T41 notify config"        "$L5PY" notify/tools/validate_notify_config.py --all
run "T47 AT-097"               "$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_no_people_datasource.py
run "T51 backup policy"        "$L5PY" access/layerb/tools/check_backup_policy.py
run "T55 acceptance offline"   "$L5PY" access/acceptance/at_runner.py --offline
run "T57 invariant map"        "$L5PY" access/tools/validate_invariant_map.py
run "T58 L2 handoff"           "$L5PY" infra/tools/validate_handoff.py
total=$((pass+fail))
echo "L5 READINESS: $pass/$total PASS"
[ "$fail" -eq 0 ] || exit 1
SH
chmod +x infra/tools/readiness.sh
```

Author `infra/READINESS.md`. It carries, and nothing else: the merge-train position (last, after L3); one row per gate above naming the task id, the gate command and its expected output; the list of ASSISTED tasks whose human half is outstanding, each with its handoff issue number; and the list of DECISION REQUIRED items routed to L0 (L5-T18 private path, L5-T21 off-VM witness, L5-T34 background-host hardware, L5-T38 orphan severity, L5-T43 phone-path custody), each with the task that raised it. It states explicitly that Lane 5 claims no part of subsystems G, H, J, O or P.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
bash infra/tools/readiness.sh
bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration
git add infra/READINESS.md infra/tools/readiness.sh
git commit -m "L5-T59: lane 5 integration readiness report and gate runner"
git push -u origin lane/5/p7-t59-readiness
gh pr create --base integration --head lane/5/p7-t59-readiness \
  --title "L5: lane 5 integration readiness (merge-train position: last)" \
  --body "Runs every lane-5 gate in one command: bash infra/tools/readiness.sh. Outstanding ASSISTED tasks and DECISION REQUIRED items are listed in infra/READINESS.md with their issue numbers. Lane 5 claims no part of subsystems G, H, J, O or P."
```

**Acceptance criteria**

| # | Criterion | Proving command | Unambiguous output |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Every gate passes | `bash infra/tools/readiness.sh \| tail -1` | `L5 READINESS: 17/17 PASS` |
| 2 | The runner fails closed on any gate | `bash infra/tools/readiness.sh >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"` | `rc=0` |
| 3 | Seventeen gates are declared | `grep -c '^run "' infra/tools/readiness.sh` | `17` |
| 4 | All five DECISION REQUIRED items are listed | `grep -c 'DECISION REQUIRED' infra/READINESS.md` | `5` |
| 5 | No foreign path was touched | `bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration` | `LANE GUARD PASS` |

**SELF-VERIFY**
```bash
set -euo pipefail
bash infra/tools/readiness.sh | tail -1
bash infra/tools/readiness.sh >/dev/null 2>&1; echo "rc=$?"
grep -c '^run "' infra/tools/readiness.sh
grep -c 'DECISION REQUIRED' infra/READINESS.md
bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration
```
Expected output (exact, five lines):
```
L5 READINESS: 17/17 PASS
rc=0
17
5
LANE GUARD PASS
```
**STOP** if any gate fails. Do not comment a gate out of `readiness.sh`, do not lower a count, and do not open the integration PR with a failing gate — Lane 5 merges last precisely so that nothing downstream absorbs its defects. Open a blocker with `TASK=L5-T59` naming every failing gate and the task that owns it.

## 14. Closing notes for the executor

1. **Order is not advisory.** P6 sits after P2 because §90.3's Layer B split needs the host, the private path and the encrypted volume to exist first, and because *no Layer B evidence may be generated before this separation exists* (§90.3) — that clause gates the phase, not a preference about sequencing.
2. **Six tasks are ASSISTED and you execute none of their console steps.** L5-T15, L5-T16, L5-T23, L5-T45, L5-T53 and L5-T56. For each you produce the artifact, commit it, open the handoff issue, and stop. If a privileged credential is ever offered to you — an organisation-owner token, a registry credential, a cloud console login, a webhook URL, key material, or the reconciler credential — do not use it, and record the offer in a blocker.
3. **Five decisions belong to L0**, not to you: the private network path mechanism (L5-T18), the off-VM dead-man witness (L5-T21), the background/inference host hardware (L5-T34), the orphan severity mapping (L5-T38), and the custody location of the filled phone-escalation path (L5-T43). If a task seems to require a sixth, that is a STOP.
4. **Subsystems G, H, J, O and P are unassigned in PARTITION v1.** Lane 5 states the dependency and routes it to L0; it never claims the subsystem, never stubs it, and never widens a Lane 5 file to cover it. AT-108, AT-109, the Hermes cage configuration files, the background-host egress override, the background-window stop unit and the local inference endpoint service are out of Lane 5 entirely.
5. **You never write outside `access/**`, `infra/**`, `ops-vm/**`, `notify/**`, `assets/**`.** Not once, not for a one-line fix, not for a test fixture, not to make a schedule work. `bash infra/tools/lane_guard_check.sh integration` before every commit.
6. **Every number in this file is derived from the spec, not from a preference.** Nine SLO objectives, eight push events, six §90.4 comparison surfaces against two comparison targets, five secret tiers, five machine credentials, eleven self-view items, sixteen asset classes, twelve classified invariants. If a SELF-VERIFY count disagrees with the spec text you printed with `sed`, recount from the spec — never edit the assertion.
7. **When a shortcut and a safety rule disagree in this lane, the safety rule wins and the shortcut becomes a blocker issue.** This lane holds the access model, the fifth secrets tier and the people layer; a relaxed gate here is not a small defect.

**End of L5-06.** Work the §4 table top to bottom. Verify with each task's SELF-VERIFY. Gate with §12 (L5-T59). Stop with §3.
